\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

\delta
is a morphism
c_{\operatorname{id}_{Z}}\to e
in
\mathfrak{E}
, which then corresponds to the desired morphism
(u,z,\gamma^{-1})\colon\operatorname{id}_{z}\to k_{e}
in
\mathfrak{M}
. Precisely,
(u,z,\gamma^{-1})
is given by
K(\delta)\circ\eta_{\operatorname{id}_{Z}}
. It is then straightforward to prove that this works, making the associated pasting into an invertible null 2-cell.

For the 2-dimensional universal property of the
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel, consider
u,v\colon Z\to K(e)
and
\lambda\colon k_{e}\circ u{\mathrel{\to}}k_{e}\circ v
such that the associated 2-cell
\Xi^{\lambda}
is a null 2-cell. We need to find a 2-cell
\mu\colon u{\mathrel{\to}}v
such that
k_{e}\ast\mu=\lambda
. Equivalently, we need
\mu
such that
(\mu,\lambda)
is a 2-cell in
\mathfrak{M}

from

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(e)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k_{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k_{e}\circ u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{id}^{u}}

to

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(e)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k_{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k_{e}\circ v}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{id}^{v}}

The equivalence of categories

{\mathfrak{M}}\left({\operatorname{id}_{z}},\hskip 0.43057pt{k_{e}}\right)\simeq{\mathfrak{E}}\left({c_{\operatorname{id}_{z}}},\hskip 0.43057pt{e}\right)

over
{\mathfrak{L}}\left({Z},\hskip 0.43057pt{A}\right)
gives a bijection on homsets

{{\mathfrak{M}}\left({\operatorname{id}_{z}},\hskip 0.43057pt{k_{e}}\right)}\left({\operatorname{id}^{u}},\hskip 0.43057pt{\operatorname{id}^{v}}\right)\overset{}{\cong}{{\mathfrak{E}}\left({c_{\operatorname{id}_{z}}},\hskip 0.43057pt{e}\right)}\left({\varepsilon_{e}\circ C(\operatorname{id}^{u})},\hskip 0.43057pt{\varepsilon_{e}\circ C(\operatorname{id}^{v})}\right)

over
{{\mathfrak{L}}\left({Z},\hskip 0.43057pt{A}\right)}\left({k_{e}\circ u},\hskip 0.43057pt{k_{e}\circ v}\right)
. So it suffices to construct a 2-cell
\sigma\colon\widehat{\epsilon_{e}}\circ\widehat{C(\operatorname{id}^{u})}{\mathrel{\to}}\widehat{\epsilon_{e}}\circ\widehat{C(\operatorname{id}^{v})}
such that
(\lambda,\sigma)
is a 2-cell in
\mathfrak{M}
from
\varepsilon_{e}\circ C(\operatorname{id}^{u})
to
\varepsilon_{e}\circ C(\operatorname{id}^{v})
. Since
\Xi^{\lambda}
is a null 2-cell by assumption, there exists
\overline{\lambda}\colon\widehat{K(\theta_{f})}\circ u{\mathrel{\to}}\widehat{K(\theta_{f})}\circ v
such that
\Xi^{\lambda}=k_{\operatorname{id}_{B}}\ast\overline{\lambda}
. Thus
(\overline{\lambda},f\ast\lambda)
is a 2-cell in
\mathfrak{M}
from
K(\theta_{f})\circ\operatorname{id}^{u}
to
K(\theta_{f})\circ\operatorname{id}^{v}
. Considering
C(\overline{\lambda},f\ast\lambda)
, one can obtain a 2-cell
(e\ast\lambda,\tau)
from the filled-in square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C(k_{e})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C(\operatorname{id}_{z})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k_{e}\circ u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{\operatorname{id}_{z}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{k_{e}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\epsilon_{e}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{C(\operatorname{id}^{u})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m\circ\widehat{\epsilon_{e}}\circ\widehat{C(\operatorname{id}^{u})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C(\operatorname{id}^{u})}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{e}}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{id}}

to the analogous one with
v
in place of
u
. By the 2-dimensional part of the diagonal lifting property, we obtain the desired 2-cell
\sigma
that works. So we obtain
\mu
such that
k_{e}\ast\mu=\lambda
. And such
\mu
is unique because
k_{e}
is faithful, as
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{M})
is
(1,1)
-proper. So
K(e)=(K(e)\xrightarrow{k_{e}}A)
is an
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel of
f
.

Dually,
C(m)=(B\xrightarrow{c_{m}}C(m))
is an
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel of
f
. Indeed, it is surely a 2-cokernel of
f
with respect to the 2-ideal
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
constructed using
C(\operatorname{id}_{A})
, and we observed above that the 2-ideal
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
is equivalent to
\mathcal{N}
. So that
C(m)
is also an
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel of
f
. We have thus proved that
\mathfrak{L}
has all
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernels and
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernels. Notice then that, since
e
has trivial
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{M})
-factorization
e=\operatorname{id}\circ e
, we have that
k_{e}
is also an
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel of
e
. And dually
c_{m}
is also an
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel of
m
. In particular we also have that
c_{k_{e}}
is an
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel of the
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel of
e
.

Since
K
and
C
give a biequivalence over
\mathfrak{L}
, there exists an equivalence
\epsilon_{e}
in
\mathfrak{E}
between
C(K(e))
and
e
. This gives in particular an equivalence
\widehat{\epsilon}_{e}\colon C(k_{e})\simeq Q
in
\mathfrak{L}
and an isomorphic 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C(k_{e})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\epsilon_{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{k_{e}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\simeq
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\epsilon}_{e}}

in
\mathfrak{L}
. We then conclude that
e
is an
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel, and precisely the
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel of the
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel of
e
. Moreover, it is straightforward to see that the structure isomorphic 2-cell that exhibits such cokernel is given, up to an invertible null 2-cell, by the structure isomorphic 2-cell of the
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel of
e
. Notice that the proof of this uses Theorem
2.28
, to build the structure 2-cell of the
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel
c_{k_{e}}
of
k_{e}
from the structure 2-cell given by
c_{k_{e}}
being the
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
-2-cokernel of
k_{e}
. Dually,
m
is the
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel of the
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel of
m
, with structure isomorphic 2-cell similarly good.

We conclude that
f
factorizes up to isomorphism as an
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel followed by an
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel. We can also show that
\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{M}
coincide with the classes of all
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernels and all
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernels respectively. Indeed, we already know that every morphism in
\mathcal{M}
is an
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel. Given then an
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel
t\colon T\to A
, say of
f
, then since also
k_{e}
is an
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel of
f
there exists an equivalence
i\colon T\simeq K(e)
and an isomorphic 2-cell
k_{e}\circ i\cong t
. Whence
t\in\mathcal{M}
because
k_{e}\in\mathcal{M}
, by the axioms of factorization system on a 2-category. And dually for
\mathcal{E}
.

Thanks to this, we deduce that every
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel is an
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel of its
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel, and that every
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel is an
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel of its
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel. Indeed, we had already shown that this holds for morphisms in
\mathcal{M}
and in
\mathcal{E}
respectively.

It remains to prove that
\mathcal{N}
is a closed 2-ideal. We show that
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernels
m\colon Q\to A
reflect null morphisms. So consider
s\colon D\to Q
such that
m\circ s
is isomorphic to a null morphism
n\colon D{\mathrel{\to}}A
, via an isomorphic 2-cell
\delta
. Then by the universal property of the
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel
c_{\operatorname{id}_{D}}
of
\operatorname{id}_{D}
, there exist
w\colon C(\operatorname{id}_{D})\to A
and an isomorphic
2
-cell as on the left below such that the pasting on the right below is a null 2-cell
\Gamma
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C(\operatorname{id}_{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{\operatorname{id}_{D}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hskip 6.02777pt\beta}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hskip 6.02777pt\delta^{-1}}
\cong
\circ
\circ
\circ
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hskip 6.02777pt\nu}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hskip 6.02777pt\gamma}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{\operatorname{id}_{D}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists w}

By the diagonal lifting property of the factorization system
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{M})
, there exist
d\colon C(\operatorname{id}_{D})\to Q
and isomorphic
2
-cells

and
\tau\colon m\circ d\cong w
such that

Since
c_{\operatorname{id}_{D}}
is isomorphic to a null morphism
y
, say via
\beta
, as it is the
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel of an identity,
\sigma
(pasted with
\beta
and
\nu
) provides an isomorphic 2-cell
\psi
between
s
and a null morphism. It is then straightforward to show that
\psi
satisfies the required axiom written in Definition
2.19
, pasting
\Gamma
with the invertible null 2-cell given by
\tau\ast y
and an invertible null 2-cell given by the pseudofunctoriality of
N
. Thus
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernels reflect null morphisms.

We now show that
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernels
m
reflect null 2-cells. So consider a
2
-cell
\mu\colon s{\mathrel{\to}}s^{\prime}\colon D{\mathrel{\to}}Q
such that
m\ast\mu
(pasted with appropriate
\nu
’s) is a null 2-cell. We can then produce, as shown earlier,
w,\gamma,\Gamma,d,\sigma,\tau
starting from
s
and analogous
w^{\prime},\gamma^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime},d^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime},\tau^{\prime}
starting from
s^{\prime}
. As
s
and
s^{\prime}
are null, we can also choose
\tau
and
\tau^{\prime}
to be identities. Pasting
\Gamma^{-1}
with
m\ast\mu
and
\Gamma^{\prime}
, we obtain a null 2-cell which induces a unique 2-cell
\theta\colon w{\mathrel{\to}}w^{\prime}\colon C(\operatorname{id}_{D})\to A
such that
\theta\ast\circ_{\operatorname{id}_{D}}=\gamma^{\prime}\circ m\ast\mu\circ\gamma^{-1}
. But then, by the 2-dimensional diagonal lifting property applied to
\gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}
, the pair of 2-cells
(\mu,\theta)
induces a 2-cell
\iota\colon d{\mathrel{\to}}d^{\prime}\colon C(\operatorname{id}_{D})\to Q
such that
\mu
coincides with
{\sigma^{\prime}}^{-1}\circ\iota\ast c_{\operatorname{id}_{D}}\circ\sigma
. It is straightforward to see that the latter is a null 2-cell. We conclude that
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernels reflect null 2-cells. Dually, we obtain that
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernels coreflect null morphisms and null 2-cells. So
\mathcal{N}
is a closed 2-ideal.

We prove
(ii){\mathrel{\to}}(i)
. We define
\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{M}
to be the classes of all
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernels and all
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernels respectively. We will denote as
K(e)\xrightarrow{k_{e}}A
the
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel of a morphism
e\colon A\twoheadrightarrow Q
in
\mathcal{E}
, and as
B\xrightarrow{c_{m}}C(m)
the
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel of a morphism
m\colon Q\rightarrowtail B
in
\mathcal{M}
. By Proposition
2.11
, every morphism in
\mathcal{E}
is cofaithful and every morphism in
\mathcal{M}
is faithful. We prove that
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{M})
is a factorization system on
\mathfrak{L}
, and thus a
(1,1)
-proper one. By assumption, every morphism
f
in
\mathfrak{L}
factorizes up to isomorphism as a morphism in
\mathcal{E}
followed by a morphism in
\mathcal{M}
. It is then easy to see that if
m\colon Q\to B
is an
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel and
i\colon R\simeq Q
is an equivalence, then
m\circ i
is an
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel, of the same morphism. And also that if
m
is an
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel, say of
g
with structure isomorphic 2-cell
\alpha
, and
\ell
is isomorphic to
m
, via
\psi
, then
l
is as well an
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel of
g
, with structure isomorphic 2-cell
\alpha\circ g\ast\psi
. Dual conditions then hold for
\mathcal{E}
as well.

We prove the orthogonality condition for
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{M})
. So let
e\in\mathcal{E}
be the
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel
D\twoheadrightarrow R
of a morphism
g\colon C\to D
with structure isomorphic 2-cell
\beta
, and
m\in\mathcal{M}
be the
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel
Q\rightarrowtail A
of a morphism
f\colon A\to B
with structure isomorphic 2-cell
\alpha
. And consider an isomorphic 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}

Then
f\circ t\circ e
is isomorphic to a null morphism, via

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q}
.
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\circ
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\circ
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{-1}}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu}

But
e
coreflects null morphisms, so there exists an isomorphic 2-cell
\psi\colon f\circ t\cong\widehat{\psi}
with
\widehat{\psi}
a null morphism such that
\nu\circ\psi\ast e
coincides with the pasting above, up to an invertible null 2-cell.
\psi
then induces
d\colon R\to Q
and
\delta
as below, by the universal property of the
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel
m
, making the following pasting into an invertible null 2-cell:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B}}
\circ
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists d}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\circ
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists\delta}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\circ
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi^{-1}}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

It is then straightforward to see that

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q}
.
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

induces an isomorphic 2-cell
\sigma\colon s{\mathrel{\to}}d\circ e
such that

This concludes the 1-dimensional part of the orthogonality condition. For the 2-dimensional part, consider squares
(s,t,\varphi)
and
(s^{\prime},t^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime})
, filled by
(d,\sigma,\rho)
and
(d^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})
respectively. Let then
(\lambda,\mu)
be a 2-cell between such squares. We apply the 2-dimensional universal property of the
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel
m
to the 2-cell
\rho^{\prime-1}\circ\mu\circ\rho
. It is straightforward to see that this 2-cell gives indeed a null 2-cell, thanks to the fact that
e
coreflects null 2-cells. It is then induced a 2-cell
\iota\colon d{\mathrel{\to}}d^{\prime}
such that

And the other axiom required to
\iota
holds because
m
is faithful. We conclude that
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{M})
is a factorization system on the 2-category
\mathfrak{L}
.

We define
K\colon\mathfrak{E}\to\mathfrak{M}
as follows. Given
e\colon A\twoheadrightarrow Q
, we send it to its
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel
K(E)\xrightarrow{k_{e}}A
. Given a morphism

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda}

in
\mathfrak{E}
, we send it to the square given by
(\widehat{K(\lambda)},a,K(\lambda))
induced as below by the
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel
k_{e^{\prime}}
, making the following pasting into an invertible null 2-cell:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{K(e)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{K(e^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q^{\prime}}}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{-1}}
\circ
\circ
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists\widehat{K(\lambda)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k_{e}}
\circ
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists K(\lambda)}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k_{e^{\prime}}}
\circ
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda^{-1}}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{\prime}}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e^{\prime}}

Given a 2-cell
(\xi,\zeta)\colon(a,q,\lambda){\mathrel{\to}}(a^{\prime},q^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime})\colon e\to e^{\prime}
in
\mathfrak{E}
, we send it to the 2-cell
([K(\xi,\zeta)],\xi)
, where
[K(\xi,\zeta)]\colon\widehat{K(\lambda)}{\mathrel{\to}}\widehat{K(\lambda^{\prime})}
is the unique 2-cell induced by the 2-dimensional universal property of the
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel
k_{e^{\prime}}
starting from the 2-cell
K(\lambda^{\prime})\circ\xi\ast k_{e}\circ K(\lambda)^{-1}
. It is straightforward to show that
K
is a normal pseudofunctor over
\mathfrak{L}
. Dually, we have that
C\colon\mathfrak{M}\to\mathfrak{E}
defined by taking
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernels is a normal pseudofunctor over
\mathfrak{L}
.

We prove that
K
and
C
give a biequivalence over
\mathfrak{L}
. It suffices to construct pseudonatural transformations
\eta\colon\operatorname{Id}{\mathrel{\to}}K\circ C
and
\epsilon\colon C\circ K{\mathrel{\to}}\operatorname{Id}
over
\mathfrak{L}
such that the components of
\eta
and
\varepsilon
are equivalences. Consider
m\colon Q\rightarrowtail B
. By assumption,
m
is an
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel of its
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel, with structure isomorphic 2-cell given, up to invertible null 2-cell, by the structure isomorphic 2-cell of the
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel of
m
. We define the component
\eta_{m}
of
\eta
on
m
as the square (actually, triangle)
(\widehat{\eta_{m}},\operatorname{id},\eta_{m})
induced by the universal property of the
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel
k_{c_{m}}
, making the following pasting into an invertible null 2-cell:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C(m)}}
\circ
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists\widehat{\eta_{m}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\circ
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists\eta_{m}^{-1}}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k_{c_{m}}}
\circ
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta^{-1}}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{m}}

where
\beta
is given by the cokernel
c_{m}
of
m
and
\alpha
is given by the kernel
k_{c_{m}}
of
c_{m}
. Since both
m
and
k_{c_{m}}
are
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernels of
c_{m}
, we deduce that
\widehat{\eta_{m}}
is an equivalence. It is straightforward to see that this implies that the morphism
\eta_{m}
in
\mathfrak{M}
is an equivalence in
\mathfrak{M}
. Given a morphism

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda}

in
\mathfrak{M}
, we define the structure 2-cell

of

on it as the isomorphic 2-cell

in
\mathfrak{M}
where
[\eta_{\lambda}]\colon\widehat{K(C(\lambda))}\circ\widehat{\eta_{m}}{\mathrel{\to}}\widehat{\eta_{m^{\prime}}}\circ q
is the unique isomorphic 2-cell such that
k_{c_{m^{\prime}}}\ast[\eta_{\lambda}]
is equal to

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{K(c_{m})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{K(c_{m^{\prime}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{K(c_{m^{\prime}})}}
.
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{K(C(\lambda))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k_{c_{m}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k_{c_{m^{\prime}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\eta_{m}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\eta_{m^{\prime}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k_{c_{m^{\prime}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(C(\lambda))}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{m}}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda^{-1}}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{m^{\prime}}^{-1}}
\cong

It is straightforward to see that such an isomorphic 2-cell
[\eta_{\lambda}]
can be induced by the 2-dimensional universal property of the
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel
k_{c_{m^{\prime}}}
, by construction of
\eta_{m}
,
\eta_{m^{\prime}}
,
C(\lambda)
and
K(C(\lambda))
. And it is straightforward to prove that
\eta
is a pseudonatural transformation over
\mathfrak{L}
, thanks to the uniqueness of
[\eta_{\lambda}]
and the fact that
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernels are faithful. Dually, we have a pseudonatural equivalence
\epsilon\colon C\circ K{\mathrel{\to}}\operatorname{Id}
over
\mathfrak{L}
. And we conclude that
K
and
C
give a biequivalence over
\mathfrak{L}
.
∎

Corollary 3.5
.

Let
\mathfrak{L}
be a 2-pointed 2-category, with a fixed choice
0
of a representative for a bizero object. The following conditions are equivalent:

(
i
)

\mathfrak{L}
satisfies condition
(i)
of Theorem
3.4
with
K
and
C
sending identities to morphisms from
0
and into
0
respectively;

(
ii
)

\mathfrak{L}
satisfies condition
(ii)
of Theorem
3.4
with
\mathcal{N}
given by the 2-ideal
(0)
canonically associated to
\mathfrak{L}
.

Proof.

We prove
(i){\mathrel{\to}}(ii)
. The 2-ideal
\mathcal{N}
constructed in the proof of Theorem
3.4
is contained in the 2-ideal
(0)
. It is straightforward to see that
\mathcal{N}
is equivalent to
(0)
, in the sense of Definition
2.25
, thanks to the universal property of the bizero object. Whence 2-kernels and 2-cokernels for the two 2-ideals are the same, by Theorem
2.28
. Finally, it is straightforward to show that
\mathfrak{L}
satisfies condition
(ii)
of Theorem
3.4
with respect to the 2-ideal
(0)
, knowing that it satisfies it with respect to the 2-ideal
\mathcal{N}
.

We prove
(ii){\mathrel{\to}}(i)
. It is easy to see that
0
gives
(0)
-2-kernels and
(0)
-2-cokernels of identities. We can thus choose in the construction of
K
and
C
of Theorem
3.4
to send identities to morphisms from
0
and into
0
respectively.
∎

Since the 1-dimensional analogue of Theorem
3.4
characterizes Grandis exact categories, we propose the following definition. Recall that the pointed version of Grandis exact categories is known as Puppe exact category.

Definition 3.6
.

We call a 2-category
\mathfrak{L}

Grandis 2-exact
if it satisfies one of the two equivalent conditions of Theorem
3.4
. A 2-pointed 2-category
\mathfrak{L}
is
Puppe 2-exact
if it satisfies one of the two equivalent conditions of Corollary
3.5
.

In the next section we will compare our notion of Puppe exactness in dimension 2 with the existing literature.

Corollary 3.7
.

In a Grandis 2-exact (or Puppe 2-exact) 2-category
\mathfrak{L}
, every morphism
f
factorizes up to isomorphism as the
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel of its
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel followed by the
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel of its
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{k_{f}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k_{c_{f}}}

Proof.

By definition of Grandis 2-exact 2-category, every morphism
f
factorizes as an
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel
e
followed by an
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel
m
. Since every
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel is an
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel of its
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel, we obtain that
e=c_{k_{e}}
; and dually
m=k_{c_{m}}
. By the proof of Theorem
3.4
, the
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel
k_{e}
of
e
coincides with the
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel of
f
, and the
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel
c_{m}
of
m
coincides with the
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel of
f
. Whence

f=m\circ e=k_{c_{m}}\circ c_{k_{e}}=k_{c_{f}}\circ c_{k_{f}}.

Analogously for a Puppe 2-exact (2-pointed)
\mathfrak{L}
.
∎

In fact, we can prove the following.

Proposition 3.8
.

Let
\mathfrak{L}
be a 2-category equipped with a 2-ideal
\mathcal{N}
of null morphisms and null 2-cells, such that
\mathfrak{L}
has all
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernels and
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernels and
\mathcal{N}
is a closed 2-ideal. Then every morphism
f
in
\mathfrak{L}
has a three-pieces factorization up to isomorphism as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C(k_{f})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(c_{f})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{k_{f}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k_{c_{f}}}
\cong

where
c_{k_{f}}
is the
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel of the
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel of
f
and
k_{c_{f}}
is the
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel of the
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel of
f
.

Proof.

By the universal property of the
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel
c_{k_{f}}
(with structure 2-cell
\delta
), there exist
u\colon C(k_{f})\to B
and
\mu\colon f{\mathrel{\to}}u\circ c_{k_{f}}
such that the pasting

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{K(f)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C(k_{f})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hskip 6.02777pt\delta}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hskip 6.02777pt\alpha^{-1}}
\cong
\circ
\circ
\circ
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k_{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hskip 6.02777pt\nu}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hskip 6.02777pt\mu}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{k_{f}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists u}

is an invertible null 2-cell, where
\alpha
is the structure 2-cell of the
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel
k_{f}
of
f
. Then
c_{f}\circ u\circ c_{k_{f}}
is isomorphic to null, pasting
\mu^{-1}
with the structure 2-cell
\beta
of the
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel
c_{f}
. Since
\mathcal{N}
is a closed 2-ideal, the
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel
c_{k_{f}}
coreflects null morphisms, and thus
c_{f}\circ u
is isomorphic to a null morphism via a nice invertible 2-cell
\psi
. We can then apply the universal property of the
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel
k_{c_{f}}
(with structure 2-cell
\gamma
) to induce
z\colon C(k_{f})\to K(c_{f})
and
\xi\colon u{\mathrel{\to}}k_{c_{f}}\circ z
such that the pasting

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C(k_{f})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{K(c_{f})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C(f)}}
\circ
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists z}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\circ
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists\xi}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k_{c_{f}}}
\circ
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi^{-1}}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{f}}

is an invertible null 2-cell. We have thus obtained a three-pieces factorization of
f
as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C(k_{f})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(c_{f})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{k_{f}}}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k_{c_{f}}}

∎

Remark 3.9
.

Dually to the proof of Proposition
3.8
, we also obtain a three-pieces factorization of
f
as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C(k_{f})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K(c_{f})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{k_{f}}}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k_{c_{f}}}

applying first the universal property of the
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel
k_{c_{f}}
and then the universal property of the
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel
c_{k_{f}}
.

It is straightforward to prove that there exists an isomorphic 2-cell

using that
k_{c_{f}}
reflects null 2-cells since
\mathcal{N}
is a closed 2-ideal.

Remark 3.10
.

In
[
4
]
, Dupont described the problem of finding a three-pieces factorization of morphisms in dimension 2, using 2-cokernels and 2-kernels. Here we presented a solution to this problem, adding the assumption that the 2-ideal is closed. Notice that in the proof of Proposition
3.8
we needed that
c_{k_{f}}
coreflects null morphisms rather than just, for example, weakly coreflects null morphisms, since in general

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{K(f)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C(f)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k_{f}}
\circ
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{-1}}
\circ
\circ
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\circ
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{f}}

need not be an invertible null 2-cell, where
\alpha
and
\beta
are the structure 2-cells of
k_{f}
and
c_{f}
respectively. The relation between the closedness condition of a 2-ideal and Dupont’s work is explored in next section.

Corollary 3.11

(of Theorem
3.4
)
.

In a Grandis 2-exact (or Puppe 2-exact) 2-category
\mathfrak{L}
, the morphisms
z
of the three-pieces factorizations of morphisms described in Proposition
3.8
are equivalences, so that every morphism
f
factorizes up to isomorphism as the
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel of its
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel followed by the
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel of its
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel.

Proof.

We already proved in Corollary
3.7
that every morphism
f
factorizes as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{k_{f}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k_{c_{f}}}

It is straightforward to prove that
k_{c_{f}}\colon Q\to B
coincides (up to isomorphism) with the induced morphism
u
constructed in the proof of Proposition
3.8
, so that
u
is an
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel. Dually,
c_{k_{f}}\colon A\to Q
coincides (up to isomorphism) with the induced morphism
v
described in Remark
3.9
, and
v
is thus an
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel. By Theorem
3.4
,
\mathfrak{L}
has a factorization system given by all
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernels and all
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernels. The equation

of Remark
3.9
then shows that
z
is the lifting given by such factorization system that connects two different factorizations of
f
(up to iso) as an
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel followed by an
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel. And we conclude that
z
is an equivalence.
∎

Remark 3.12
.

We can view Corollary
3.7
and Corollary
3.11
as saying that a Grandis 2-exact (or Puppe 2-exact) 2-category satisfies a 2-categorical generalization of the first isomorphism theorem from algebra.

We conclude this section by showing that our theory captures as examples all Grandis exact categories in the 1-dimensional sense, and thus all abelian categories.

Proposition 3.13
.

A locally discrete 2-category
\mathfrak{L}
is Grandis 2-exact (respectively, Puppe 2-exact) if and only if its underlying 1-category is Grandis exact (in the usual 1-dimensional sense).

Proof.

In a locally discrete 2-category, all 2-cells are identities and thus all pasting diagrams are commutative diagrams. A 2-ideal of null morphisms and null 2-cells is precisely an ideal of null morphisms on the underlying 1-category of
\mathfrak{L}
. Moreover, 2-kernels and 2-cokernels are precisely kernels and cokernels in the usual 1-dimensional sense. Indeed, the 1-dimensional universal property of a 2-kernel gives the existence condition and the 2-dimensional universal property gives the uniqueness condition of the usual universal property of kernels. Reflecting null morphisms in the 2-dimensional sense means precisely reflecting null morphisms in the ordinary sense; while all morphisms automatically reflect null 2-cells. So closed 2-ideals on
\mathfrak{L}
are precisely closed ideals in the ordinary sense. Notice that here there is no difference between weakly reflecting null morphisms and reflecting null morphisms. It is now straightforward to see that
\mathfrak{L}
satisfies condition
(ii)
of Theorem
3.4
precisely when its underlying 1-category is Grandis exact. Notice that every kernel being the kernel of its cokernel, as well as the dual condition, is automatic in dimension 1.

For the Puppe exact case, notice that a bizero object in
\mathfrak{L}
is precisely a zero object in the underlying 1-category of
\mathfrak{L}
. Moreover, the canonical 2-ideal
(0)
associated to a 2-pointed locally discrete 2-category is exactly the canonical ideal
(0)
associated to the underlying 1-category (which is pointed). So we conclude.
∎

We then have the following corollary.

Corollary 3.14
.

Every Grandis exact category in the ordinary sense is a Grandis 2-exact locally discrete 2-category.

Every Puppe exact category in the ordinary sense, and thus every abelian category, is a Puppe 2-exact locally discrete 2-category.

Remark 3.15
.

For a locally discrete 2-category
\mathfrak{L}
, condition
(i)
of Theorem
3.4
precisely means that there exists an orthogonal factorization system
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{M})
on the underlying 1-category of
\mathfrak{L}
and an equivalence over
\mathfrak{L}
(which becomes an isomorphism when taking isomorphism classes) between the fibration of quotients relative to
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{M})
and the opfibration of subobjects relative to
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{M})
(with
K
and
C
functors). Indeed, the 1-dimensional orthogonality condition gives the existence of liftings and the 2-dimensional orthogonality condition gives uniqueness of liftings. The pseudo arrow category of
\mathfrak{L}
is just the usual arrow category of the underlying 1-category of
\mathfrak{L}
, seen as a locally discrete 2-category. And a biequivalence over
\mathfrak{L}
is the same as an equivalence over the underlying 1-category of
\mathfrak{L}
.

Notice that the condition of having a factorization system which is
(1,1)
-proper is void for a locally discrete 2-category
\mathfrak{L}
.

Our proof of Theorem
3.4
gives then a new proof for the 1-dimensional result of the second author and Weighill in
[
7
]
, and shows that the properness condition that they ask is redundant.

4.
Comparison with the literature and applications

In this section, we compare our 2-dimensional notion of Puppe exactness (Definition
3.6
) with the notions developed by Dupont (
[
4
]
) and Nakaoka (
[
9
,
10
]
) in the pointed case. To do so, we introduce a slightly weaker notion of 2-dimensional Puppe exactness in a natural way and prove that this generalizes most of the theory and the examples developed in
[
4
,
9
,
10
]
. Furthermore, we show that such weaker notion of Puppe exactness still implies a biequivalence over
\mathfrak{L}
given by taking kernels and cokernels. So we deduce that many examples in the literature enjoy this fundamental exactness property.

We start by recalling the 2-dimensional Puppe exactness properties studied by Dupont and Nakaoka, in the particular case of (strictly described) categories enriched over pointed groupoids. We refer the reader to the references written below for the notions of
0
-monomorphism,
0
-faithful, fully
0
-faithful, strictly described category enriched over
\mathrm{Gpd}^{\ast}
(that is, over pointed groupoids), locally SCG category, root, pip and dual notions. Following the convention 3.2 of
[
10
]
, we consider locally SCG categories without requiring condition (LS3+).

Proposition 4.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{L}
be a strictly described category enriched over
\mathrm{Gpd}^{\ast}
with a zero object (see
[
4
]
). Then for a morphism
f
in
\mathfrak{L}
the following are equivalent:

(
i
)

f
is 0-faithful;

(
ii
)

f
reflects null 2-cells.

Moreover, also the following are equivalent:

(
i
)

f
is fully 0-faithful;

(
ii
)

f
reflects null morphisms and null 2-cells.

Proof.

Since
\mathfrak{L}
is strictly described, all morphisms through the zero object are the special zero morphism
0
between the right source and target. The unique null 2-cell between parallel null morphisms is then the identity. It is now straightforward to conclude from
[
4
, Definition 78, Definition 80]
.
∎

Definition 4.2

(
[
4
, Definition 165]
)
.

An
abelian
\mathrm{Gpd}^{\ast}
-category
is a category
\mathfrak{L}
enriched over
\mathrm{Gpd}^{\ast}
with a zero object, finite products and coproducts such that

(
i
)

\mathfrak{L}
has all 2-kernels and 2-cokernels (see Remark
2.17
);

(
ii
)

every 0-monomorphism is a 2-kernel of its 2-cokernel (with appropriate structure 2-cell), and dually every 0-epimorphism is a 2-cokernel of its 2-kernel;

(
iii
)

fully 0-faithful arrows and 0-monomorphisms are stable under pushout, and dually fully 0-cofaithful arrows and 0-epimorphisms are stable under pullback.

Definition 4.3

(
[
9
, Definition 3.7]
)
.

A
relatively exact
\mathrm{Gpd}^{\ast}
-category
is a locally SCG category
\mathfrak{L}
(where SCG stands for symmetric categorical group) such that:

(
i
)

\mathfrak{L}
has all 2-kernels and 2-cokernels;

(
ii
)

every faithful morphism is a 2-kernel of its 2-cokernel (with appropriate structure 2-cell), and dually every cofaithful morphism is a 2-cokernel of its 2-kernel.

Definition 4.4

(
[
4
, Proposition 179, Definition 183]
)
.

A
2-Puppe-exact
\mathrm{Gpd}^{\ast}
-category
(á la Dupont) is a category
\mathfrak{L}
enriched over
\mathrm{Gpd}^{\ast}
with a zero object such that

(
i
)

\mathfrak{L}
has all 2-kernels and 2-cokernels;

(
ii
)

every 0-faithful arrow is a 2-kernel of its 2-cokernel (with appropriate structure 2-cell), and dually every 0-cofaithful arrow is a 2-cokernel of its 2-kernel;

(
iii
)

every fully 0-faithful arrow is a root of its copip (canonically), and dually every fully 0-cofaithful arrow is a coroot of its pip.

A
2-abelian
\mathrm{Gpd}^{\ast}
-category
is a 2-Puppe-exact
\mathrm{Gpd}^{\ast}
-category (á la Dupont) that has all finite products and coproducts.

Remark 4.5
.

The above notions studied by Dupont and Nakaoka are compared in
[
10
]
. It is shown there that

(
i
)

every strictly described 2-abelian
\mathrm{Gpd}^{\ast}
-category is a relatively exact
\mathrm{Gpd}^{\ast}
-category;

(
ii
)

every relatively exact
\mathrm{Gpd}^{\ast}
-category is an abelian
\mathrm{Gpd}^{\ast}
-category.

In order to compare our 2-dimensional notion of Puppe exactness with the above definitions, we introduce a slightly weaker version of Definition
3.6
. In particular, we need to weaken the concept of closed ideal, using the weaker form of reflection of null morphisms that we introduced in Definition
2.21
. Recall also Proposition
2.24
.

Definition 4.6
.

Let
\mathfrak{L}
be a 2-category and
\mathcal{N}=(N,\nu)
be a 2-ideal of null morphisms and null 2-cells in
\mathfrak{L}
. Assume that
\mathfrak{L}
has all
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernels and
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernels. We call
\mathcal{N}
a
weakly closed 2-ideal
if all
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernels weakly reflect null morphisms and reflect null 2-cells, and all
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernels weakly coreflect null morphisms and coreflect null 2-cells.

Remark 4.7
.

Clearly every closed 2-ideal is weakly closed.

Recall from Proposition
2.24
that all
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernels weakly reflecting null morphisms is equivalent to all
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernels weakly coreflecting null morphisms.

Proposition 4.8
.

Let
\mathfrak{L}
be a 2-pointed 2-category, with a fixed choice
0
of a representative for the bizero object. The 2-ideal
(0)
canonically associated to
\mathfrak{L}
is weakly closed.

Proof.

By Proposition
2.24
, all 2-kernels weakly reflect null morphisms. Indeed all null morphisms factor through
0
, which is a null object, and it is easy to see that condition (ii) of Proposition
2.24
is satisfied. We prove that 2-kernels reflect null 2-cells. So let
\mu
be a 2-cell as on the left hand side below such that
k\ast\mu
is a null 2-cell:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{=}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists!\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists!\cong}

By uniqueness of the 2-cells
k\circ i{\mathrel{\to}}k\circ i^{\prime}\colon 0\to A
, the pasting on the right hand side above is equal to

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists!\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists!\cong}

Since
k
is faithful, we conclude that
\mu
is null. Dually for 2-cokernels.
∎

Remark 4.9
.

The 2-ideal
(0)
canonically associated to a 2-pointed 2-category is not always a closed 2-ideal. Moreover, it does not seem to automatically satisfy any of the properties listed in condition
(ii)
of Theorem
3.4
. With regards to kernels not always being kernels of their cokernels, a counter-example can be found in
[
4
, Proposition 97]
.

Definition 4.10
.

Let
\mathfrak{L}
be a 2-pointed 2-category, with a fixed choice
0
of a representative for the bizero object. We say that
0
is a
strong bizero object
if for every
D\xrightarrow{t}0\xrightarrow{i}K
and
D\xrightarrow{t^{\prime}}0\xrightarrow{i^{\prime}}K
morphisms in
\mathfrak{L}
, there is a unique 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\exists!}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}}

In other words,
0
is a strong bizero object if all 2-cells between null morphisms are null 2-cells.

Proposition 4.11
.

Let
\mathfrak{L}
be a 2-pointed 2-category with a strong bizero object
0
. Then the 2-ideal
(0)
canonically associated to
\mathfrak{L}
is a closed 2-ideal.

Proof.

The proof is clear after Proposition
4.8
.
∎

We are now ready to introduce a slightly weaker version of Definition
3.6
.

Definition 4.12
.

A
weakly Grandis 2-exact
2-category is a 2-category
\mathfrak{L}
equipped with a 2-ideal
\mathcal{N}
of null morphisms and null 2-cells such that

-

\mathfrak{L}
has all
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernels and
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernels;

-

\mathcal{N}
is a weakly closed 2-ideal;

-

every
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel
m
is an
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel of its
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel
c_{m}
, with structure isomorphic 2-cell between
c_{m}\circ m
and a null morphism given up to an invertible null 2-cell by the
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel
c_{m}
of
m
; and dually for
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernels;

-

every morphism
f
in
\mathfrak{L}
factorizes up to isomorphism as an
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel followed by an
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 2-\operatorname{coker}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 2-\operatorname{ker}}

A
weakly Puppe 2-exact
2-category is a 2-pointed 2-category
\mathfrak{L}
, with a fixed choice
0
of a representative for a bizero object, that satisfies the conditions above of weakly Grandis 2-exactness with respect to the 2-ideal
(0)
canonically associated to
\mathfrak{L}
. Weakly closedness of the 2-ideal
(0)
is automatic by Proposition
4.8
.

Remark 4.13
.

The only difference between weakly Grandis (respectively, weakly Puppe) 2-exactness and Grandis (respectively, Puppe) 2-exactness is that we only require the 2-ideal to be weakly closed rather than closed. Remember that weakly reflection of null morphisms arose naturally in Section
2
, when noticing that two equivalent characterizations of closed ideal in dimension 1 are not equivalent anymore in dimension 2.

Notice that, of course, a 2-pointed 2-category with a strong bizero object is weakly Puppe 2-exact precisely when it is Puppe 2-exact.

A large part of condition
(i)
of Theorem
3.4
still holds for weakly Grandis 2-exact 2-categories. One of the most delicate aspects is the orthogonality condition of the factorization system. We will need to weaken such condition as follows.

Definition 4.14
.

Let
\mathfrak{L}
be a 2-category and let
\mathcal{N}
be a 2-ideal of null morphisms and null 2-cells. We say that
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{M})
is a
relatively orthogonal cokernel-kernel factorization system
on
\mathfrak{L}
if the following conditions are satisfied:

(
i
)

every morphism in
\mathcal{E}
is an
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel and every morphism in
\mathcal{M}
is an
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel;

(
ii
)

every morphism in
\mathfrak{L}
factors up to isomorphism as a morphism in
\mathcal{E}
followed by a morphism in
\mathcal{M}
;

(
iii
)

if
m\in\mathcal{M}
and
i
is an equivalence then
m\circ i\in\mathcal{M}
, and dually for
\mathcal{E}
;

(

Moreover, given squares
(s,t,\varphi)
and
(s^{\prime},t^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime})
, filled by
(d,\sigma,\rho)
and
(d^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime},\rho^{\prime})
respectively and a 2-cell
(\lambda,\mu)
between such squares, there exists a unique 2-cell
\iota\colon d{\mathrel{\to}}d^{\prime}
such that

Remark 4.15
.

A relatively orthogonal cokernel-kernel factorization system is a factorization system with a weaker orthogonality condition relative to the 2-ideal
\mathcal{N}
. Indeed, condition (v) of Definition
4.14
is the usual orthogonality required for a 2-dimensional orthogonal factorization system restricted to the squares from
e\in\mathcal{E}
to
m\in\mathcal{M}
that are compatible with the structure 2-cells that present
e
and
m
as a cokernel and a kernel respectively.

Remark 4.16
.

Given a relatively orthogonal cokernel-kernel factorization system
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{M})
on a 2-category
\mathfrak{L}
, we still have the 2-functors
\operatorname{dom}\colon\mathfrak{E}\to\mathfrak{L}
and
\operatorname{cod}\colon\mathfrak{M}\to\mathfrak{L}
introduced in Theorem
3.3
, that consider the 2-quotients and the 2-subobjects relative to
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{M})
. However, they might not be weak 2-(op)fibrations anymore.

We now show that a large part of the proof of
(ii){\mathrel{\to}}(i)
in Theorem
3.4
can still be carried out when we relax the assumption of a closed 2-ideal to that of a weakly closed 2-ideal.

Theorem 4.17
.

Let
\mathfrak{L}
be a weakly Grandis 2-exact 2-category, with respect to a weakly closed 2-ideal
\mathcal{N}
. Then the classes
\mathcal{E}
of all
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernels and
\mathcal{M}
of all
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernels form a relatively orthogonal cokernel-kernel factorization system on
\mathfrak{L}
such that there exists a biequivalence over
\mathfrak{L}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{L}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{dom}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
\simeq
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{cod}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}

between the 2-functors
\operatorname{dom}
and
\operatorname{cod}
associated to
(\mathcal{E},\mathcal{M})
, with
K
and
C
normal pseudofunctors.

Analogously for
\mathfrak{L}
weakly Puppe 2-exact.

Proof.

We start by proving that
(\mathcal{E}.\mathcal{M})
is a relatively orthogonal cokernel-kernel factorization system. Condition (i) and (ii) of Definition
4.14
are satisfied by construction and conditions (iii) and (iv) are straightforward to prove using the properties of
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernels and
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernels. To prove (iv), consider a square
(s,t,\varphi)
between
e\in\mathcal{E}
and
m\in\mathcal{M}
as below, where
m
is the
\mathcal{N}
-2-kernel of
f\colon A\to B
with structure 2-cell
\alpha
and
e
is the
\mathcal{N}
-2-cokernel of
g\colon C\to D
with structure 2-cell
\beta
, such that the 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}
\cong
\circ
\circ
\circ
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\cong
\circ
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{-1}}
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

is an invertible null 2-cell. Notice that given this invertible null 2-cell we can break up the
\nu
relative to
s\circ g
into the pasting of the one relative to
s
with the one relative to
g
up to an isomorphic null 2-cell and so the 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B}}
\circ
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\cong
\cong
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu}
\circ
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{-1}}
\cong

is compatible with
\beta
. Hence, the fact that
e
weakly coreflects null morphisms induces a 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\cong
\circ
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\varphi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

with the same properties as the one of the proof of Theorem
3.4
. From here on the same argument of the proof of Theorem
3.4
allows us to conclude the 1-dimensional part of the orthogonality property. The proof of the 2-dimensional part of the orthogonality property remains precisely the same as in Theorem
3.4
since we still have that
e
coreflects null 2-cells. So
(\mathcal{E}.\mathcal{M})
is a relatively orthogonal cokernel-kernel factorization system. Moreover, the existence of the biequivalence between
K
and
C
follows from the same argument of the proof of Theorem
3.4
, since that part of the proof does not use that
\mathcal{N}
is a closed ideal (not even the weak closedness of
\mathcal{N}
is is needed there).
∎

We now compare our theory with the 2-dimensional Puppe exactness properties studied by Dupont and Nakaoka. Notice first that all the considered notions of Puppe 2-exactness require the 2-category to have all 2-kernels and 2-cokernels. There is then some variation on which morphisms are required to be 2-kernels of their 2-cokernels and dually 2-cokernels of their 2-kernels.

Remark 4.18
.

It is almost true that every abelian
\mathrm{Gpd}^{\ast}
-category is weakly Puppe 2-exact. Indeed, in an abelian
\mathrm{Gpd}^{\ast}
-category, 0-monomorphisms coincide with 2-kernels, by
[
4
, Corollary 158, Proposition 166]
. So the first three out of four conditions of weakly Puppe 2-exactness (see Definition
4.12
) are satisfied. Moreover, by
[
4
, Corollary 160]
, every abelian
\mathrm{Gpd}^{\ast}
-category has a factorization system given by the epimorphisms (in Dupont’s terminology) and the fully 0-faithful arrows. While epimorphisms are precisely the 2-cokernels in an abelian
\mathrm{Gpd}^{\ast}
-category, fully 0-faithful arrows are not necessarily 2-kernels. So in general not every abelian
\mathrm{Gpd}^{\ast}
-category is weakly Puppe 2-exact, nor the converse holds. However, the stronger Nakaoka’s notion of relatively exactness also ensures that fully 0-faithful arrows are 2-kernels. We obtain the following theorem.

Theorem 4.19
.

For
\mathfrak{L}
a locally SCG category, the following are equivalent:

(
i
)

\mathfrak{L}
is a relatively exact
\mathrm{Gpd}^{\ast}
-category;

(
ii
)

\mathfrak{L}
is weakly Puppe 2-exact, every faithful morphism is a 2-kernel and every cofaithful morphism is a 2-cokernel.

Proof.

We prove
(i){\mathrel{\to}}(ii)
. By Remark
4.18
and Remark
4.5
, we know that every relatively exact
\mathrm{Gpd}^{\ast}
-category satisfies the first three out of four conditions of weakly Puppe 2-exactness, because every abelian
\mathrm{Gpd}^{\ast}
-category does so. Moreover, again by Remark
4.18
and Remark
4.5
, we know that every morphism factorizes up to isomorphism as a 2-cokernel followed by a fully 0-faithful morphism. But fully 0-faithful morphisms are 0-faithful and, by Lemma 5.2 of
[
10
]
, in a relatively exact
\mathrm{Gpd}^{\ast}
-category 0-faithful morphisms are faithful. Moreover by definition of relatively exact
\mathrm{Gpd}^{\ast}
-category, every faithful morphism is a 2-kernel and thus we conclude that every morphism factorizes up to isomorphism as a 2-cokernel followed by a 2-kernel.

We now prove
(ii){\mathrel{\to}}(i)
. By assumption if
\mathfrak{L}
is weakly Puppe 2-exact it has all 2-kernels and all 2-cokernels. Moreover, in a weakly Puppe 2-exact 2-category every 2-kernel is a 2-kernel of its 2-cokernel and so, thanks to the assumption that every faithful monomorphism is a 2-kernel, we conclude that every faithful morphism is a 2-kernel of its 2-cokernel. And dually for cofaithful morphisms.
∎

Remark 4.20
.

In the context of locally SCG categories, the distance between our weakly 2-Puppe exactness and Nakaoka’s relatively exactness is thus the request that all faithful morphisms, i.e. all 2-dimensional monomorphisms, are 2-kernels and all cofaithful morphisms, i.e. all 2-dimensional epimorphisms, are 2-cokernels. In dimension 1, if every morphism factorizes as a cokernel followed by a kernel, then automatically all monomorphisms are kernels and all epimorphisms are cokernels. But the same argument does not extend to dimension 2.

Theorem 4.21
.

Let
\mathfrak{L}
be a 2-Puppe-exact
\mathrm{Gpd}^{\ast}
-category (á la Dupont). Then
\mathfrak{L}
is weakly Puppe 2-exact.

Proof.

By definition of 2-Puppe-exact
\mathrm{Gpd}^{\ast}
-category (á la Dupont),
\mathfrak{L}
has all 2-kernels and all 2-cokernels with respect to the ideal
(0)
. Moreover, the ideal
(0)
is weakly closed by Proposition
4.8
.

We prove that every 2-kernel is a 2-kernel of its 2-cokernel with appropriate structure 2-cell. Let
m
be a 2-kernel. Thanks to the universal property of the 2-kernel
m
, is faithful and thus it is 0-faithful. But then, by Proposition 179 of
[
4
]
,
m
is canonically the 2-kernel of its 2-cokernel. Analogously, the dual property holds for 2-cokernels.

Finally, the fact that every morphism in
\mathfrak{L}
factorizes up to isomorphism as a 2-cokernel followed by a 2-kernel follows from Proposition 179 of
[
4
]
.
∎

Remark 4.22
.

Our notion of weakly Puppe 2-exactness thus generalizes both Nakaoka’s relatively exactness and Dupont’s 2-Puppe-exactness, and as a consequence also Dupont’s 2-abelianness. So all the examples of relatively exact
\mathrm{Gpd}^{\ast}
-category studied in
[
9
]
and all the examples of 2-Puppe-exact (or 2-abelian)
\mathrm{Gpd}^{\ast}
-category (á la Dupont) studied in
[
4
]
are examples of our theory.

The following diagram condenses the comparison between the considered 2-dimensional notions of Puppe exactness:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{\mathrm{Gpd}^{\ast}\text{-category}}{\text{abelian}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{\mathrm{Gpd}^{\ast}\text{-category}}{\text{relatively exact}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{\mathrm{Gpd}^{\ast}\text{-category}}{\text{2-abelian}}}}
weakly Puppe 2-exact
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underset{\text{(\'{a} la Dupont)}}{\underset{\mathrm{Gpd}^{\ast}\text{-category}}{\text{2-Puppe-exact}}}}}

Our work applies the theory of (2-dimensional) fibrations, as well as that of factorization systems, to abstractly deduce how to define Grandis and Puppe exactness in dimension 2, as opposed to trying to extract the right conditions from inspecting the examples. This paper also sheds new light on the 2-dimensional cohomology theory started by Dupont and Nakaoka.
